<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
55 + a                              &= 82 \\\\
a                                   &= 82 - 55 \\\\
a                                   &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

Missing number $= 27$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}

a                                   &= 45 + 23 \\\\
a                                   &= 68
\end{aligned}
$

Missing number $= 68$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$60 \div \boxed{\phantom{45}} = 12$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
60 \div a                           &= 12 \\\\
60                                  &= 12 \times a \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {60} {12}\\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {12} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel{12}} {\cancel{12}} \\\\
a                                   &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

Missing number $= 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<emptyline>

60                                  &= 12 \times a \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {60} {12}\\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {12} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel{12}} {\cancel{12}} \\\\
a                                   &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Missing number $= 5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$0.75 \div \dfrac{3}{4} = \boxed{\phantom{45}}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {0.75} {\dfrac {3} {4}} \\\\
&= \dfrac {75} {100} \times \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {75 \times 4} {100 \times 3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 25 \times 4} {4 \times 25 \times 3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times \cancel{25} \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4} \times \cancel{25} \times \cancel{3}} \\\\
&= 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {0.75} {\dfrac {3} {4}} \\\\
&= \dfrac {75} {100} \times \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {75 \times 4} {100 \times 3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 25 \times 4} {4 \times 25 \times 3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times \cancel{25} \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4} \times \cancel{25} \times \cancel{3}} \\\\
&= 1
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question question'>

Divide $3112$ by $8$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{3112}{8} \\\\
&= \dfrac{389 \times 8}{8} \\\\
&= \dfrac{389 \times \cancel{8}}{\cancel{8}} \\\\
&= 389
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

$389$

</div>

&= 389
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Angus and his six friends have collected $756$ football stickers, which they all share out equally.  
How many stickers do they each get?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Stickers each person will get}                 &= \dfrac {\text{Total stickers}} {\text{Total people}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {756} {7} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 108} {7} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {\cancel{7} \times 108} {\cancel{7}} \\\\
                                              &= 108
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$108$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Stickers each person will get}                 &= \dfrac {\text{Total stickers}} {\text{Total people}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {756} {7} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 108} {7} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {\cancel{7} \times 108} {\cancel{7}} \\\\
                                              &= 108
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$108$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Today is Emma's $29 ^ \text{th}$ birthday. How many months old is she?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Emma's age in month}                    &= 29 \times 12 \\\\
                                              &= 348
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$348$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Emma's age in month}                    &= 29 \times 12 \\\\
                                              &= 348
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$348$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Amar works for $45$ weeks each year. 
His total cost of travelling to and from work is $\pounds 630$ each year. 
How much is that per week?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amar's travelling cost per week}        &= \dfrac {\text{Total cost each year}} {\text{No. of weeks}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {630} {45} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {14 \times 45} {45} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {14 \times \cancel{45}} {\cancel{45}} \\\\
                                              &= \pounds 14
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Amar's travelling cost per week}        &= \dfrac {\text{Total cost each year}} {\text{No. of weeks}} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {630} {45} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {14 \times 45} {45} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {14 \times \cancel{45}} {\cancel{45}} \\\\
                                              &= \pounds 14
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Here is a part of a number line. Write the two missing numbers in the boxes.

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-7-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-7-subquestion-2-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-7-subquestion-2-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{1} ^ \text{st} \text{ missing number}     &= 2 - \dfrac{1}{4} - \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
                                                &= 2 - 0.5 \\\\
                                                &= 1.5 \\\\
\text{2} ^ \text{nd} \text{ missing number}     &= 2 + 0.25 + 0.25 \\\\
                                                &= 2 + 0.5 \\\\
                                                &= 2.5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.5$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$2.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{1} ^ \text{st} \text{ missing number}     &= 2 - \dfrac{1}{4} - \dfrac{1}{4} \\\\
                                                &= 2 - 0.5 \\\\
                                                &= 1.5 \\\\
\text{2} ^ \text{nd} \text{ missing number}     &= 2 + 0.25 + 0.25 \\\\
                                                &= 2 + 0.5 \\\\
                                                &= 2.5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

</code></pre>

\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>6D3071DE-0CCA-4999-A569-D3A93983E3C0</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1:1:9</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tsimpleword
</li>
<li>
tpercentage
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

At Redville Primary School, $16$ of the teachers travel to work by car, $6$ travel by bus and the other $3$ walk. 

|  Car | Bus | Walk |
|:----:|:---:|:----:|
| $16$ | $6$ |  $3$ |

What *percentage* of the teachers travel by car?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage of teachers}       &= \dfrac {\text{Teachers who travel by car}} {\text{Total teachers}} \times 100 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {16} {16 + 6 + 3} \times 100 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {16 \times 100} {25} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {16 \times 4 \times 25} {25} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {16 \times 4 \times \cancel{25}} {\cancel{25}} \\\\
                                    &= 16 \times 4 \\\\
                                    &= 64 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$64 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage of teachers}       &= \dfrac {\text{Teachers who travel by car}} {\text{Total teachers}} \times 100 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {16} {16 + 6 + 3} \times 100 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {16 \times 100} {25} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {16 \times 4 \times 25} {25} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {16 \times 4 \times \cancel{25}} {\cancel{25}} \\\\
                                    &= 16 \times 4 \\\\
                                    &= 64 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$64 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Clarissa buys the sunglasses and a sun hat. 
How much change, in pounds, does she get from a $\pounds 10$ note?

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-10-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-10-subquestion-1-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-10-subquestion-1-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Change}               &= 10 - 5.85 - 3.29 \\\\
                            &= 10 - 9.14 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 0.86
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 0.86$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Change}               &= 10 - 5.85 - 3.29 \\\\
                            &= 10 - 9.14 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 0.86
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Jo and Aisha bought identical jeans from a market stall. 

Jo got $10 \%$ discount of the full price. 

Aisha got $15 \%$ discount of the full price. Jo paid $\pounds 1.20$ more than Aisha. 

What was the full price of the jeans?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the full price of the jeans $= p$

Jo got $10 \%$ discount and Aisha got $15 \%$ discount. So Jo paid $15 - 10 = 5 \%$ more of the full price.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \% \ \text{of} \ p        &= 1.20 \\\\
\dfrac {5} {100} \times p   &= 1.20 \\\\
p                           &= \dfrac {1.20 \times 100} {5} \\\\
p                           &= \dfrac {1.20 \times 20 \times 5} {5} \\\\
p                           &= \dfrac {1.20 \times 20 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
p                           &= 1.20 \times 20 \\\\
p                           &= \pounds 24
\end{aligned}
$

Full price of the jeans $= \pounds 24$

</div>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Sale price = Original price} - \text{Discount } \% \times \text{Original price}$


ABBR: $\text {Sale price = (100 - Discount})\ \% \times \text{Original price}$


Let the full price of the jeans $= p$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(p - 10 \% \ \text{of} \ p)  - (p - 15 \% \ \text{of} \ p)  &= 1.20 \\\\
p - 10 \% \ \text{of} \ p  - p + 15 \% \ \text{of} \ p      &= 1.20 \\\\
15 \% \ \text{of} \ p - 10 \% \ \text{of} \ p               &= 1.20 \\\\
5 \% \ \text{of} \ p                                        &= 1.20 \\\\
\dfrac {5} {100} \times p                                   &= 1.20 \\\\
p                                                           &= \dfrac {1.20 \times 100} {5} \\\\
p                                                           &= \dfrac {1.20 \times 20 \times 5} {5} \\\\
p                                                           &= \dfrac {1.20 \times 20 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
p                                                           &= 1.20 \times 20 \\\\
p                                                           &= \pounds 24
\end{aligned}
$

Full price of the jeans $= \pounds 24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the full price of the jeans $= p$

p                           &= 1.20 \times 20 \\\\
p                           &= \pounds 24
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A film starts at $6{:}45 \ \text{pm}$ and lasts for $2 \ \text{hours } 35 \ \text{minutes}$.

At what time will it finish?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{End time} &= 6{:}45  + 2 \ \text{hours } 35 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                 &= 9{:}20 \ \text{pm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9{:}20 \ \text{pm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{End time} &= 6{:}45  + 2 \ \text{hours } 35 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                 &= 9{:}20 \ \text{pm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A coach left London Victoria $5 \ \text{minutes}$ late, at $13{:}45$, 
and arrived in Banbury $14 \ \text{minutes}$ early, at $17{:}05$. 
How long should the journey have taken if the coach had left and arrived on time?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Original time to leave}    &= 13{:}45 - 5 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                  &= 13{:}40  \\\\
\text {Original time to arrive}   &= 17{:}05 + 14 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                  &= 17{:}19 \\\\
\text {Duration of journey}       &= 17{:}19 - 13{:}40 \\\\
                                  &= 3 \ \text {hours} \ 39 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \ \text {hours} \ 39 \ \text{minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Original time to leave}    &= 13{:}45 - 5 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                  &= 13{:}40  \\\\
\text {Original time to arrive}   &= 17{:}05 + 14 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                  &= 17{:}19 \\\\
\text {Duration of journey}       &= 17{:}19 - 13{:}40 \\\\
                                  &= 3 \ \text {hours} \ 39 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3 \ \text {hours} \ 39 \ \text{minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Chris runs round a track at a speed of $6 \ \text{km/hour}$.

Dave runs round the same track at a speed of $9 \ \text{km/hour}$.

When Chris has run $18$ laps, how many laps has Dave run?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Ratio of their speed}            &= \dfrac {9} {6} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 3} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 3} {\cancel{3} \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
\text {Laps ran by Dave}                &= \dfrac {3} {2} \ \text {of} \ 18 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3} {2} \times 18 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 18} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
                                        &= 3 \times 9 \\\\
                                        &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Ratio of their speed}            &= \dfrac {9} {6} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 3} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 3} {\cancel{3} \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
\text {Laps ran by Dave}                &= \dfrac {3} {2} \ \text {of} \ 18 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3} {2} \times 18 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 18} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 9 \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
                                        &= 3 \times 9 \\\\
                                        &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$27$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many sixths are there in $3 \dfrac{1}{3}$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Dividing fractions is the same as multiplying by the reciprocal (inverse) of the second fraction.


$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3 \dfrac{1}{3} \div \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac {10} {3} \div \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac {10} {3} \times 6 \\\\
&= \dfrac {10 \times 6} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {10 \times 2 \times 3} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {10 \times 2 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
&= 10 \times 2 \\\\
&= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_divide_by_fraction_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3 \dfrac{1}{3} \div \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac {10} {3} \div \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac {10} {3} \times 6 \\\\
&= \dfrac {10 \times 6} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {10 \times 2 \times 3} {3} \\\\
&= \dfrac {10 \times 2 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
&= 10 \times 2 \\\\
&= 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the value of $2 + \dfrac{1}{2} + 3 + \dfrac{1}{3} + 6 + \dfrac{1}{6}$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 + \dfrac {1} {2} + 3 + \dfrac {1} {3} + 6 + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= 11 + \dfrac {1 \times 3} {2 \times 3} + \dfrac {1 \times 2} {3 \times 2} + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= 11 + \dfrac {3} {6} + \dfrac {2} {6} + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= 11 + \dfrac {3 + 2 + 1} {6} \\\\
&= 11 + \dfrac {6} {6} \\\\
&= 11 + \dfrac {\cancel{6}} {\cancel{6}} \\\\
&= 11 + 1 \\\\
&= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 + \dfrac {1} {2} + 3 + \dfrac {1} {3} + 6 + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= 11 + \dfrac {1 \times 3} {2 \times 3} + \dfrac {1 \times 2} {3 \times 2} + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= 11 + \dfrac {3} {6} + \dfrac {2} {6} + \dfrac {1} {6} \\\\
&= 11 + \dfrac {3 + 2 + 1} {6} \\\\
&= 11 + \dfrac {6} {6} \\\\
&= 11 + \dfrac {\cancel{6}} {\cancel{6}} \\\\
&= 11 + 1 \\\\
&= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Two numbers have a difference of $3$ and a sum of $27$. What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number $= a$

Second number $= a + 3$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + (a + 3)                 &= 27 \\\\
2a + 3                      &= 27 \\\\
2a                          &= 27 - 3 \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {24} {2} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {2 \times 12} {2} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 12} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                           &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The first number} &= 12 \\\\
\text{The second number} &= 12 + 3 \\\\
                         &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12, 15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number $= a$
<emptyline>
Second number $= a + 3$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + (a + 3)                 &= 27 \\\\

2a                          &= 27 - 3 \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {24} {2} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {2 \times 12} {2} \\\\
a                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 12} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                           &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The first number} &= 12 \\\\
\text{The second number} &= 12 + 3 \\\\
                         &= 15
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12, 15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Start with the number $20$, then multiply by $4$, then add $16$, 
then divide by $12$, then find three-quarters of your answer. 

What number do you get?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Required value}  &= (20 \times 4 + 16) \div 12 \times \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {80 + 16} {12} \times \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {96 \times 3} {12 \times 4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {12 \times 4 \times 2 \times 3} {12 \times 4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 4 \times 2 \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                        &= 2 \times 3 \\\\
                        &= 6
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Required value}  &= (20 \times 4 + 16) \div 12 \times \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {80 + 16} {12} \times \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {96 \times 3} {12 \times 4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {12 \times 4 \times 2 \times 3} {12 \times 4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 4 \times 2 \times 3} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                        &= 2 \times 3 \\\\
                        &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='question question'>

The total length of the edges of a cube is $240 \ \text{cm}$. What is the length of one edge?

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-16-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-16-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-16-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Total number of edges in a cube $= 12$

Let the edge of the cube $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \times a &= 240 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {240} {12} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {12 \times 20} {12} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac { \cancel {12} \times 20} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
a           &= 20 \ \text{cm} 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20 \ \text{cm} $

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Total number of edges in a cube $= 12$
<emptyline>
Let the edge of the cube $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
12 \times a &= 240 \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {240} {12} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac {12 \times 20} {12} \\\\
a           &= \dfrac { \cancel {12} \times 20} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
a           &= 20 \ \text{cm} 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20 \ \text{cm} $
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Here is some information about three different squares:

The *area* of Vikram's square is $64 \ \text{cm}^2$. 

The *length of a side* of Kim's square is $64 \ \text{cm}$.

The *perimeter* of Ali's square is $64 \ \text{cm}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working placeholder'>

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

</div>
</div>

</div>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Who has the largest square?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of Vikram's square}             &= 64 \ \text{cm}^2 \\\\
\text {Area of Kim's square}                &= 64 \times 64 \\\\
                                            &= 4096 \ \text{cm}^2 \\\\
\text {Let the side of the Ali's square}    &= a \\\\
4 \times a                                  &= 64 \\\\
a                                           &= \dfrac {64} {4} \\\\
a                                           &= \dfrac {4 \times 16} {4} \\\\
a                                           &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 16} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a                                           &= 16 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Area of Ali's square}                &= a \times a \\\\
                                            &= 16 \times 16 \\\\
                                            &= 256 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

Ali has the largest square.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Ali

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_square_expanded
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_square
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

                                            &= 4096 \ \text{cm}^2 \\\\
\text {Let the side of the Ali's square}    &= a \\\\
4 \times a                                  &= 64 \\\\
a                                           &= \dfrac {64} {4} \\\\
a                                           &= \dfrac {4 \times 16} {4} \\\\
a                                           &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 16} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a                                           &= 16 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Area of Ali's square}                &= a \times a \\\\
                                            &= 16 \times 16 \\\\
                                            &= 256 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Ali has the largest square.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$

a                                           &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 16} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a                                           &= 16 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Area of Ali's square}                &= a \times a \\\\
                                            &= 16 \times 16 \\\\
                                            &= 256 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

Vikram has the smallest square.

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Lollipops cost $12 \ \text{p}$ each, or a pack of $3$ costs $30 \ \text{p}$. 

I have $\pounds 2$ to spend. 

What is the maximum number of lollipops I can buy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of packs}             &= \dfrac {\pounds 2} {30 \ \text{p}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {200 \ \text{p}} {30 \ \text{p}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {20 \cancel {0}} {3 \cancel {0}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {20} {3} \\\\
                                    &= 6 \ \text {remainder } 2 \\\\
                                    &\approx 6 \\\\
\text {Money left}                  &= 200 - 6 \times 30 \\\\
                                    &= 200 - 180 \\\\
                                    &= 20 \ \text{p} \\\\
\text {Extra lollipops we can buy}  &= \dfrac {20} {12} \\\\
                                    &= 1 \ \text {remainder} \ 8 \\\\
                                    &\approx 1 \\\\
\text {Total lollipops}             &= 6 \times 3 + 1 \\\\
                                    &= 18 + 1 \\\\
                                    &= 19
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of packs}             &= \dfrac {\pounds 2} {30 \ \text{p}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {200 \ \text{p}} {30 \ \text{p}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {20 \cancel {0}} {3 \cancel {0}} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {20} {3} \\\\
                                    &= 6 \ \text {remainder } 2 \\\\
                                    &\approx 6 \\\\
\text {Money left}                  &= 200 - 6 \times 30 \\\\
                                    &= 200 - 180 \\\\
                                    &= 20 \ \text{p} \\\\
\text {Extra lollipops we can buy}  &= \dfrac {20} {12} \\\\
                                    &= 1 \ \text {remainder} \ 8 \\\\
                                    &\approx 1 \\\\
\text {Total lollipops}             &= 6 \times 3 + 1 \\\\
                                    &= 18 + 1 \\\\
                                    &= 19
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Every $100 \ \text{g}$ of brown bread contains $6 \ \text{g}$ of fibre. 

A small loaf weighs $400 \ \text{g}$ and has $10$ equal slices. 

How much fibre is there in one slice?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
100 \ \text {g of bread}    &= 6 \ \text {g of fibre} \\\\
100 \times 4                &= 6 \times 4 \\\\
400 \ \text {g of bread}    &= 24 \ \text {g of fibre}
\end {aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Fibre in one slice of bread} &= \dfrac {24} {10} \\\\\
                                    &= 2.4 \ \text {g}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2.4 \ \text {g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
100 \ \text {g of bread}    &= 6 \ \text {g of fibre} \\\\
100 \times 4                &= 6 \times 4 \\\\
400 \ \text {g of bread}    &= 24 \ \text {g of fibre}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Fibre in one slice of bread} &= \dfrac {24} {10} \\\\\
                                    &= 2.4 \ \text {g}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2.4 \ \text {g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate $(9999 - 999 + 99 - 9) \div 9$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


ABBR: Using BIDMAS
,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (9999 - 999 + 99 - 9) \div 9 \\\\
&= 9090 \div 9 \\\\
&= \dfrac {9090}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1010 \times 9}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1010 \times \cancel{9}}{\cancel{9}} \\\\
&= 1010
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1010$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
rtq_abbr_md_bidmas,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= (9999 - 999 + 99 - 9) \div 9 \\\\
&= 9090 \div 9 \\\\
&= \dfrac {9090}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1010 \times 9}{9} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1010 \times \cancel{9}}{\cancel{9}} \\\\
&= 1010
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the value of $20 \%$ of $(60)^{2}?$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>



$
\begin{aligned}
&= 20 \% \text { of } (60)^{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{20}{100} \times 60 \times 60 \\\\
&= \dfrac{20 \times 60 \times 60}{100}  \\\\
&= \dfrac{20 \times 6\cancel{0} \times 6\cancel{0}}{1\cancel{0}\cancel{0}}  \\\\
&= 20 \times 6 \times 6 \\\\
&= 720
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$720$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 20 \% \text { of } (60)^{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{20}{100} \times 60 \times 60 \\\\
&= \dfrac{20 \times 60 \times 60}{100}  \\\\
&= \dfrac{20 \times 6\cancel{0} \times 6\cancel{0}}{1\cancel{0}\cancel{0}}  \\\\
&= 20 \times 6 \times 6 \\\\
&= 720
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Find the right katex symbol.*

On the cards below, each club $\spadesuit$ has the same value and 
each spade $\spadesuit$ has the same value (but a different value to each club).

The number on each card is the total value of the symbols on that card. 

Find the value of one club.

![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-26-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-26-subquestion-1-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-1-question-26-subquestion-1-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the value of spade $= a$

Value of club $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2a + 2b             &= 20 \\\\
2a + 3b             &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve them. Let's eliminate to reduce it to just one variable.

Subtract the first equation from the second to reduce it to one variable.

$
\begin{aligned}
2a + 3b - (2a + 2b)                 &= 27 - 20 \\\\
2a + 3b - 2a - 2b                   &= 7 \\\\
b                                   &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

Value of club $= 7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the value of spade $= a$
<emptyline>
Value of club $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2a + 2b             &= 20 \\\\
2a + 3b             &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We have got $2$ variables and we have $2$ equations to solve them. Let's eliminate to reduce it to just one variable.
<emptyline>
Subtract the first equation from the second to reduce it to one variable.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2a + 3b - (2a + 2b)                 &= 27 - 20 \\\\
2a + 3b - 2a - 2b                   &= 7 \\\\
b                                   &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Value of club $= 7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g3 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>D738448C-76F7-496D-A768-F9FABB4E17F7</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1:1:27</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g3</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tage
</li>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Claire is two years older than her brother and five years younger than her sister. 
The total of their ages is $87$ years.

How old is Claire?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let Claire's age $= a$

Her brother's age $= a - 2$

Her sister's age $= a + 5$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + a - 2 + a + 5   &= 87 \\\\
3a + 3              &= 87 \\\\
3a                  &= 87 - 3 \\\\
3a                  &= 84 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {84} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {28 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {28 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                   &= 28 \ \text {years}
\end {aligned}
$

Claire's age $= 28 \ \text {years}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$28 \ \text {years}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let Claire's age $= a$
<emptyline>
Her brother's age $= a - 2$
<emptyline>
Her sister's age $= a + 5$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
a + a - 2 + a + 5   &= 87 \\\\
3a + 3              &= 87 \\\\
3a                  &= 87 - 3 \\\\
3a                  &= 84 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {84} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {28 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {28 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                   &= 28 \ \text {years}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Claire's age $= 28 \ \text {years}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$28 \ \text {years}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

# Section D
<ul class='question decimal question_type_question list_type_decimal'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>65287338-9E1E-4F7C-A6AE-8A7A4BE67CD6</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1:2:1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>

tsequence
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

Look at this pattern:

$3^{2} = 1^{2} + 8$

$4^{2} = 2^{2} + 12$

$5^{2} = 3^{2} + 16$

$6^{2} = 4^{2} + 20$

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down the next line of the pattern:

$\ldots = \ldots + \ldots$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
7^{2}   &= 5^{2} + 4 \times (7 - 1) \\\\
        &= 5^{2} + 4 \times 6 \\\\
        &= 5^{2} + 24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7^{2} = 5^{2} + 24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
7^{2}   &= 5^{2} + 4 \times (7 - 1) \\\\
        &= 5^{2} + 4 \times 6 \\\\
        &= 5^{2} + 24
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7^{2} = 5^{2} + 24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Use *the pattern* to complete this line:

$20^{2} = \ldots + \ldots$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
20^{2} &= (20-2)^{2} + 4 \times (20-1) \\\\
       &= (20-2)^{2} + 4 \times 19 \\\\
       &= 18^{2} + 76
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20^{2} = 18^{2} + 76$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
20^{2} &= (20-2)^{2} + 4 \times (20-1) \\\\
       &= (20-2)^{2} + 4 \times 19 \\\\
       &= 18^{2} + 76
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20^{2} = 18^{2} + 76$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Use *the pattern* and the fact that $249^{2} = 62001$ to find the value of $251^{2}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
251^{2} &= (251-2)^{2} + 4 \times (251-1) \\\\
        &= 249^{2} + 4 \times 250 \\\\
        &= 249^{2} + 1000 \\\\
        &= 62001 + 1000 \\\\
        &= 63001
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$63001$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
251^{2} &= (251-2)^{2} + 4 \times (251-1) \\\\
        &= 249^{2} + 4 \times 250 \\\\
        &= 249^{2} + 1000 \\\\
        &= 62001 + 1000 \\\\
        &= 63001
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$63001$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many square numbers are there from $1$ to $10000$ inclusive?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\sqrt{10000}    &= 100
\end{aligned}
$

Square numbers between $1$ to $10000 = 100$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$100$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\sqrt{10000}    &= 100
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Square numbers between $1$ to $10000 = 100$.
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The area of a right angled triangle is $32 \ \text{cm}^{2}$.

The lengths, in $\text{cm}$, of the two shorter sides are both different square numbers bigger than $1$.

What are the lengths of the two shorter sides?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of triangle} = \dfrac{1}{2} \times \text{base} \times \text{height}$


Let the height of the triangle $= h$

Let the base of the triangle $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {2} \times b \times h    &= 32 \\\\
\dfrac {b \times h} {2}             &= 32 \\\\
b \times h                          &= 32 \times 2 \\\\
b \times h                          &= 64
\end{aligned}
$

Factors of $64 = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64$

Factor pairs of $64$ that are both different square numbers $ = 4, 16$

Length of the two shorter sides are $4 \ \text {cm}$ and $16 \ \text {cm}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4 \ \text {cm} \ , 16 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_triangle_expanded
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

\dfrac {b \times h} {2}             &= 32 \\\\
b \times h                          &= 32 \times 2 \\\\
b \times h                          &= 64
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Factors of $64 = 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64$
<emptyline>
Factor pairs of $64$ that are both different square numbers $ = 4, 16$
<emptyline>
Length of the two shorter sides are $4 \ \text {cm}$ and $16 \ \text {cm}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \ \text {cm} \ , 16 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

*Use* the fact that $17 \times 18 \times 19 = 5814$

to work out the value of $34 \times 36 \times 38$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 34 \times 36 \times 38 \\\\
&= 17 \times 2 \times 18 \times 2 \times 19 \times 2 \\\\
&= 2 \times 2 \times 2 \times 17 \times 18 \times 19 \\\\
&= 8 \times 5814 \\\\
&= 46512
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$46512$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 34 \times 36 \times 38 \\\\
&= 17 \times 2 \times 18 \times 2 \times 19 \times 2 \\\\
&= 2 \times 2 \times 2 \times 17 \times 18 \times 19 \\\\
&= 8 \times 5814 \\\\
&= 46512
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Seven *consecutive* whole numbers add up to $7357$.  What is the largest of the seven numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the seven consecutive whole numbers $= a, a+1, a+2, a+3, a+4, a+5, a+6$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 1 + a + 2 + a + 3 + a + 4 + a + 5 + a + 6   &= 7357 \\\\
7a + 21                                             &= 7357 \\\\
7a                                                  &= 7357 - 21 \\\\
7a                                                  &= 7336 \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {7336} {7} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {7 \times 1048} {7} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel 7 \times 1048} {\cancel 7} \\\\
a                                                   &= 1048
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The largest whole number}     &= 1048 + 6 \\\\
                                    &= 1054
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1054$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the seven consecutive whole numbers $= a, a+1, a+2, a+3, a+4, a+5, a+6$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

7a + 21                                             &= 7357 \\\\
7a                                                  &= 7357 - 21 \\\\
7a                                                  &= 7336 \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {7336} {7} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {7 \times 1048} {7} \\\\
a                                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel 7 \times 1048} {\cancel 7} \\\\
a                                                   &= 1048
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The largest whole number}     &= 1048 + 6 \\\\
                                    &= 1054
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1054$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

When three *consecutive* whole numbers are multiplied together, the answer is $990$. What are the three numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the three consecutive whole numbers $= a-1, a, a+1$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a - 1) \times a \times (a + 1)         &= 990 \\\\
a \times (a ^ 2 - 1)                    &= 990 \\\\
a ^ 3 - a                               &= 990 \\\\
a ^ 3                                   &\approx 990 \\\\
a                                       &\approx 990 ^ {\dfrac {1} {3}} \\\\
a                                       &\approx 9
\end{aligned}
$

|  Triplets   |  Product  |
|:-----------:|:---------:|
| $7, 8, 9$   |   $504$   |
| $8, 9, 10$  |   $720$   |
| $9, 10, 11$ |   $990$   |

The three whole numbers $= 9, 10, 11$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9, 10, 11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the three consecutive whole numbers $= a-1, a, a+1$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(a - 1) \times a \times (a + 1)         &= 990 \\\\
a \times (a ^ 2 - 1)                    &= 990 \\\\
a ^ 3 - a                               &= 990 \\\\
a ^ 3                                   &\approx 990 \\\\
a                                       &\approx 990 ^ {\dfrac {1} {3}} \\\\
a                                       &\approx 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
|  Triplets   |  Product  |
|:-----------:|:---------:|
| $7, 8, 9$   |   $504$   |
| $8, 9, 10$  |   $720$   |
| $9, 10, 11$ |   $990$   |
<emptyline>
The three whole numbers $= 9, 10, 11$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9, 10, 11$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many Tinkles make one Clink?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total clinks}             &= 2 + 2 + 1 + 1 \\\\
                                &= 6 \\\\
\text{Remaining clinks}         &= 8 - 6 \\\\
                                &= 2 \\\\
\text{Total tinkles}            &= 5 + 4 + 3 + 3 \\\\
                                &= 15 \\\\
\text{Remaining tinkles}        &= 15 - 3 \\\\
                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of tinkles per clink}      &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
                                        &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

$6$ tinkles $= 1$ clink

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total clinks}             &= 2 + 2 + 1 + 1 \\\\
                                &= 6 \\\\
\text{Remaining clinks}         &= 8 - 6 \\\\
                                &= 2 \\\\
\text{Total tinkles}            &= 5 + 4 + 3 + 3 \\\\
                                &= 15 \\\\
\text{Remaining tinkles}        &= 15 - 3 \\\\
                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of tinkles per clink}      &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
                                        &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$6$ tinkles $= 1$ clink
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the answer to this subtraction.

|     | Clinks | Tinkles |
|:---:|:------:|:-------:|
|     |   $4$  |   $3$   |
| $-$ |   $2$  |   $5$   |
|     |$\ldots$| $\ldots$|


![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-4-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-4-subquestion-2-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-4-subquestion-2-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First number}                     &= 4 \ \text{clinks} + 3 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 4 \times 6 \ \text{tinkles} + 3 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 24 + 3 \\\\
                                        &= 27 \ \text{tinkles}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Second number}                    &= 2 \ \text{clinks} + 5 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 2 \times 6 \ \text{tinkles} + 5 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 12 + 5 \\\\
                                        &= 17 \ \text{tinkles}
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Subtraction}                      &= \text{First Number} - \text{Second Number} \\\\
                                        &= 27 - 17 \\\\
                                        &= 10 \\\\
                                        &= 6 + 4 \\\\
                                        &= 1 \ \text{clink} + 4 \ \text{tinkles}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First number}                     &= 4 \ \text{clinks} + 3 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 4 \times 6 \ \text{tinkles} + 3 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 24 + 3 \\\\
                                        &= 27 \ \text{tinkles}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Second number}                    &= 2 \ \text{clinks} + 5 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 2 \times 6 \ \text{tinkles} + 5 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 12 + 5 \\\\
                                        &= 17 \ \text{tinkles}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Subtraction}                      &= \text{First Number} - \text{Second Number} \\\\
                                        &= 27 - 17 \\\\
                                        &= 10 \\\\
                                        &= 6 + 4 \\\\
                                        &= 1 \ \text{clink} + 4 \ \text{tinkles}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the answer to this division.


|     | Clinks | Tinkles |
|:---:|:------:|:-------:|
| $4$ |   $7$  |   $2$   |
|     |$\ldots$| $\ldots$|


![bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-4-subquestion-3-00.png](/assets/bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-4-subquestion-3-00.png "bancrofts-school--11-plus--maths--2013--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-4-subquestion-3-00.png")



</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The dividend}                     &= 7 \ \text{clinks} + 2 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 7 \times 6 \ \text{tinkles} + 2 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 42 + 3 \\\\
                                        &= 44 \ \text{tinkles}
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The answer}                       &= \dfrac {44} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {4 \times 11} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 11} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= 11 \\\\
                                        &= 6 + 5 \\\\
                                        &= 1 \ \text{clink} + 5 \ \text{tinkles}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The dividend}                     &= 7 \ \text{clinks} + 2 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 7 \times 6 \ \text{tinkles} + 2 \ \text{tinkles} \\\\
                                        &= 42 + 3 \\\\
                                        &= 44 \ \text{tinkles}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The answer}                       &= \dfrac {44} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {4 \times 11} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 11} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= 11 \\\\
                                        &= 6 + 5 \\\\
                                        &= 1 \ \text{clink} + 5 \ \text{tinkles}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15$
</code></pre>
</div>

Here is some information about Sports Day: 

|                                  There were $4$ teams: Red, Blue, Green, Yellow                                  |      There were $6$ races: Parents race, Sack race, Egg-And-Spoon, Three-Legged race, Obstacle race, Nose Pushing race      |
|:----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------:|:---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------:|
| Blue won the Obstacle race, just beating Yellow, but Blue came last in the Egg-And-Spoon, which was won by Green |                     In the Parents, Sack and Three-Legged races, Blue came second and Green came last                       |
|                                          Everyone won at least one race                                          |                  There was exactly the same result in the Sack race as there was in the Three-legged race                   |
|                            Red came first on two occasions, with Blue second each time                           | First and third in the Nose race and Parents race were the same, but were the opposite way round in the Three-Legged race   |
|                                   Yellow came third three times and first twice                                  |                                               Red did not win the Nose race                                                 |
|                              Green came last three times, but Yellow never came last                             |             The winner of each race got $6$ points, second got $4$ points, third $3$ points, fourth $2$ points              |

<div class='workings'>
